**Source:** https://www.gulfunitedfc.com/news/what-football-scouts-actually-look-for
**Title:** The Truth About Football Academies: What Scouts Actually Look For

Every parent watching their child play football has wondered: what exactly are scouts looking for? Is it goals scored? Speed? Tricks and flicks? The ability to dominate youth matches?

The reality of talent identification in professional football academies is both more complex and more layered than most parents realise. At Gulf United FC, our coaching staff includes scouts and talent identifiers who've worked at various levels of professional football. The attributes that genuinely predict long-term success often surprise parents focused on current performance.

Here's what professional football scouts actually assess when evaluating young players, and why immediate standout performances matter less than you think.

**The Four Pillars of Player Assessment**

Professional football academies evaluate players across four interconnected areas:

1. **Technical Ability** - Football-specific skills

2. **Physical Attributes** - Athletic capabilities and potential

3. **Tactical Intelligence** - Game understanding and decision-making

4. **Psychological Qualities** - Mindset, character, and coachability

No single pillar determines selection. Scouts seek well-rounded players with foundation in all areas and exceptional potential in at least one or two.

**Technical Ability: Beyond Flashy Skills**

When parents think "technical ability," they often picture step-overs, tricks, and spectacular goals. Scouts look for something different.

**What Scouts Actually Assess:**

**First Touch Quality**

Can the player receive the ball cleanly under various pressures? Do they control it into space where their next action is easier?

The ability to receive passes under pressure and immediately be in position to play is perhaps the single most important technical skill. Players with exceptional first touch create time and space; those with poor first touch are always under pressure.

**Both Feet Capability**

Not ambidextrous perfection, but genuine comfort using both feet in match situations.

Scouts watch: Can they pass with their weak foot? Receive on it? Shoot when necessary? Players who avoid their weak foot in competitive situations limit their positional flexibility and tactical value.

**Passing Range and Accuracy**

The variety of passes a player can execute matters more than one spectacular long ball.

Scouts assess:

- Short passing accuracy (5-15 yards)

- Medium-range distribution (15-30 yards)

- Weight of pass (making it easy for teammates to receive)

- Decision-making about when to play short vs long

**Movement Quality**

How players move with and without the ball reveals enormous information.

- Running mechanics (efficiency, balance, coordination)

- Change of direction technique

- Body positioning when receiving

- Awareness of surroundings during movement

**Ball Striking Technique**

Clean contact, appropriate power for distance, accuracy. This applies to passing, shooting, and crossing.

Players with proper technique improve quickly with coaching. Those with fundamental mechanical issues require significant remedial work.

**What Matters LESS Than Parents Think:**

- Spectacular tricks (unless executing them effectively in match context)

- Goal-scoring records at youth level (particularly for non-forwards)

- Ability to dominate weaker opponents physically

- Highlight-reel moments

Scouts recognize that technically flashy players who lack fundamentals often plateau. The player with exceptional first touch but no tricks has higher ceiling than the trickster with poor fundamentals.

**Physical Attributes: Potential Over Current State**

At younger ages (U9-U14), physical maturity varies enormously. Some children have hit early growth spurts; others haven't started puberty. Scouts must identify athletic potential, not current physical advantages.

**What Scouts Actually Assess:**

**Movement Quality and Coordination**

How efficiently does the player move? Do they look comfortable in their body? Is their running technique sound?

Scouts can teach speed and strength. They cannot easily fix poor movement patterns or coordination deficiencies.

**Athleticism Indicators**

- Acceleration over first 5-10 yards

- Change of direction fluidity

- Jumping and landing mechanics

- Balance and body control

These suggest trainable athletic potential.

**Growth Potential**

For younger players, scouts consider:

- Parent height (genetic indicators)

- Current growth stage (early vs late maturer)

- Body frame and proportions

- Physical development trajectory

A currently small player from tall parents with late maturation indicators may have more long-term potential than a physically mature early developer dominating through size.

**Recovery Between Efforts**

How quickly does the player recover from sprints or intense actions? This indicates cardiovascular capacity and trainability.

**Relative Strength**

Strength relative to body size matters more than absolute strength at youth levels.

**What Matters LESS:**

- Current height or size (especially before ages 14-15)

- Immediate speed if movement quality is poor

- Physical dominance over smaller/younger opponents

- Strength without accompanying coordination

Many physically dominant youth players plateau when opponents catch up in size. The physically mature 12-year-old who relies on size often struggles when everyone is 16.

**Tactical Intelligence: The Underrated Differentiator**

This is where elite players separate from good ones. Tactical intelligence is harder to develop than technical skills or physical attributes.

**What Scouts Actually Assess:**

**Decision-Making Speed and Quality**

- Does the player know what they'll do before receiving the ball?

- Do they recognize when to dribble vs pass vs shoot?

- Do they choose appropriate actions for game situations?

The player who constantly makes good decisions with average technique exceeds the technically gifted player who makes poor choices.

**Spatial Awareness**

- Where are teammates? Opponents? Space?

- Do they check shoulders before receiving?

- Do they recognize and exploit gaps in defense?

- Can they find pockets of space to receive?

Players with exceptional awareness seem to have "more time" on the ball because they've processed information early.

**Positioning Without the Ball**

Elite players impact the game constantly, not just when they have the ball.

Scouts watch:

- Do they move to support teammates?

- Do they create passing angles?

- Do they recognize when to drop deep vs push forward?

- Do they understand defensive positioning and organization?

**Game Understanding**

- Do they recognize game moments (when to speed up, slow down, keep possession, take risks)?

- Do they understand their role within team shape?

- Can they adjust based on match situation?

**Pattern Recognition**

The ability to see developing situations, anticipate play, and position accordingly.

The player who intercepts passes isn't just fast; they recognized patterns and positioned themselves before the pass was played.

**What Matters LESS:**

- Following tactical instructions perfectly (coaching can teach this)

- Remembering complex set-piece routines

- Playing specific positions (flexibility is valuable)

Scouts distinguish between players who've been well-coached tactically and players who inherently understand space, timing, and game flow.

**Psychological Qualities: The Long-Term Predictors**

This pillar often determines who reaches their potential and who doesn't.

**What Scouts Actually Assess:**

**Coachability**

- Does the player listen to and implement coaching feedback?

- Do they accept criticism constructively?

- Can they try new techniques even when current methods feel comfortable?

- Do they ask questions to understand, not argue?

Highly talented players who resist coaching rarely develop fully. Moderately talented, highly coachable players often exceed expectations.

**Resilience and Response to Adversity**

- How do they react after mistakes?

- Do they persist when struggling?

- Can they handle being substituted or not selected?

- Do they bounce back from poor performances?

Football careers involve constant setbacks. Players who crumble under pressure or dwell on mistakes struggle at higher levels.

**Competitive Mentality**

- Do they compete in training, not just matches?

- Do they push themselves when tired?

- Do they step up in important moments?

- Do they hate losing enough to work harder?

This is different from poor sportsmanship. It's internal drive to improve and win.

**Self-Motivation**

- Do they practice outside structured training?

- Do they seek additional development opportunities?

- Is their effort consistent or dependent on external motivation?

Scouts recognize that professional development requires self-driven commitment. Players who only work when pushed rarely reach elite levels.

**Teamwork and Communication**

- Do they celebrate teammates' success?

- Do they communicate during play?

- Are they respected by peers?

- Do they prioritize team success over individual glory?

Selfish players create locker room problems regardless of talent.

**Humility and Work Ethic**

- Do they accept they have room to improve?

- Do they work on weaknesses, not just showcase strengths?

- Are they willing to do unglamorous work (recovery runs, defensive tracking)?

Arrogant youth players rarely fulfill potential. Humble, hard-working players often exceed expectations.

**What Matters LESS:**

- Confidence (this can be developed)

- Extroversion (quiet leaders exist)

- Immediate emotional control (improving with maturity is normal)

Scouts distinguish between fixable psychological traits and deep character concerns.

**Age-Specific Assessment Differences**

What scouts prioritize shifts based on player age:

**Ages 6-10:**

Focus: Fun, enthusiasm, basic coordination, willingness to try, respectful behavior

Priority: Is this child enjoying football and showing fundamental movement capability?

**Ages 10-13:**

Focus: Technical foundation, trainability, tactical understanding development, growth potential

Priority: Does this player have the technical and tactical foundation to develop?

**Ages 13-16:**

Focus: All four pillars balanced, competitive mentality, physical development trajectory

Priority: Can this player potentially reach professional level?

**Ages 16+:**

Focus: Elite-level attributes in multiple areas, professional mentality, position-specific excellence

Priority: Is this player ready for professional pathway?

**Common Parent Misconceptions**

**"My child scores lots of goals, so scouts will be interested"**

Goal-scoring at youth level often reflects physical maturity, not potential. Unless your child is a forward, goals scored matter little. Even for forwards, scouts assess chance creation, movement, finishing technique, not raw goal totals.

**"My child dominates their recreational league"**

Domination of weak competition reveals little about potential against higher-level opponents. Scouts want to see performance in competitive environments against quality opposition.

**"My child has been scouted, so they'll definitely make professional football"**

Being scouted means a club sees potential. Most scouted players don't reach professional level. It's the beginning of a journey, not a guarantee.

**"Technical skills are most important"**

Scouts seek balance. A technically brilliant player who lacks competitive mentality or game intelligence rarely succeeds professionally.

**"Early development means future success"**

Late developers often surpass early maturers. Physical advantages at age 12 disappear by 16. Scouts look beyond current physical state.

**What You Can Control vs What You Can't**

**You Can Control:**

- Your child's exposure to quality coaching

- Their attitude and work ethic development

- Multi-sport participation for athletic development

- Ensuring they play for love of game, not external pressure

- Academic support so football isn't their only option

**You Cannot Control:**

- Genetic athletic potential

- Growth timing and ultimate height

- Scouts' subjective evaluations

- Other players' development

- Club politics or selection biases

Focus energy on controllables.

**The Gulf United Scouting Philosophy**

At Gulf United FC, our identification process reflects these principles:

**We assess:**

- Long-term potential, not current peak performance

- Character and coachability alongside ability

- Fit within our development philosophy

- Balance across the four pillars

**We value:**

- Late developers with strong fundamentals

- Tactically intelligent players who make good decisions

- Coachable athletes with strong work ethic

- Players who love the game genuinely

**We recognize:**

- Early physical maturity doesn't predict future success

- Technical flashiness without substance has limited value

- Psychological qualities determine who fulfills potential

**Conclusion: Development Over Immediate Standout**

Professional scouts look beyond highlight moments and goal tallies. They assess foundation, potential, and character.

The player who seems unremarkable now but has exceptional first touch, game intelligence, coachability, and work ethic often develops into elite performer.

The physically dominant youth star who relies on size and avoids their weak foot often plateaus.

If your child aspires to high-level football, focus on:

- Developing both feet

- Improving game understanding

- Building resilient, competitive mentality

- Maintaining genuine love for football

- Creating well-rounded athletic foundation

These attributes predict long-term success more than current performance levels.

**Title:** Football Scouts: Identifying Talent

Identifying potential talent in football is both an art and a science. Football scouts play an essential role in this process, using various tools and techniques to uncover the next generation of football stars. In this blog post, we will explore how scouts evaluate players, the metrics they use, and what aspiring players can do to catch their attention.

## The Role of Football Scouts

Football scouts are responsible for observing players during matches and training sessions. They carefully assess skills, techniques, and physical attributes to determine if a player has what it takes to succeed at a higher level. Scouts often look for specific characteristics, including:

*   **Technical Skills**: This includes dribbling, passing accuracy, shooting ability, and defensive skills.

*   **Physical Attributes**: Height, speed, fitness levels, and overall athleticism are also crucial.

*   **Tactical Understanding**: Understanding the game, reading plays, and making quick decisions are vital for success in football.

## Key Qualities Football Scouts Look For

When evaluating potential talent, scouts focus on certain qualities that set exceptional players apart. Here are some of the key attributes they usually prioritise:

### 1. Technical Ability

A player’s technical ability is perhaps the most important factor for scouts. This includes ball control, dribbling, passing, and shooting proficiency. For instance, Lionel Messi’s exceptional dribbling skill and precise finishing made him a prime candidate for professional football from a young age.

### 2. Physical Conditioning

Scouts pay significant attention to a player's physicality. Speed, agility, strength, and stamina can greatly influence a player’s effectiveness on the field. Physical tests, such as sprinting and endurance drills, often accompany technical assessments during evaluation.

### 3. Mental Toughness

Mental strength encompasses a player’s attitude, work ethic, and resilience. Scouts frequently seek players who demonstrate leadership abilities on the field. A player who can maintain composure in critical situations often garners additional atte ntion.

## The Scouting Process

### 1. Initial Evaluation

Scouts often start their evaluations by observing players in local leagues, youth academies, and schools. Using a keen eye, they assess players during live matches. This often involves taking detailed notes to compare skills and performance metrics over time.

### 2. Data Analysis

In recent years, technology has transformed the way scouts operate. Many scouts now use data analytics to assess players. Metrics such as:

These statistics complement traditional scouting methods and help provide a clearer picture of a player's performance over time.

Once a scout identifies a promising player, they conduct follow-up assessments. This can include arranging for the player to participate in training sessions with a professional club or youth academy. This hands-on approach allows scouts to see how the player performs under pressure and in team environments.

## Tips for Aspiring Players

If you're an aspiring football player looking to catch the attention of scouts, there are several actionable steps you can take:

### 1. Work on Your Skills

Constantly develop your technical abilities. Dedicate time to practice shooting, passing, dribbling, and defensive tactics. Attend training camps or clinics whenever possible.

### 2. Focus on Fitness

Maintain peak physical fitness. Regular exercise, a healthy diet, and proper hydration can significantly enhance your performance on the pitch.

Purchase the booklet produced by our Head of Fitness & Nutrition here:

### 3. Showcase Your Talents

Participate in local leagues or community tournaments. Make sure to perform well consistently, as scouts often attend these games to identify fresh talent.

### 4. Create a Highlight Reel

Consider creating a video highlight reel that showcases your skills. You can share this with coaches and scouts who may be interested in recruiting you.

### 5. Join the Player Network

Include your Player Profile on the UK Football Scouting Player Network used by Managers, Coaches and Scouts from clubs of all levels of the game across the UK.

### 6. Be Professional

Show up on time for training and games, and demonstrate good sportsmanship. A positive attitude can make a lasting impression on both coaches and scouts.

By following these tips, you can enhance your chances of being noticed by the talent scouts. Whether you are managing the complexities of the game or trying to keep up with your peers, consistency and hard work are essential.

## The Future of Scouting

As football continues to evolve, so does the scouting profession. With advancements in technology and data analytics, the role of scouts is becoming more sophisticated. They can now access detailed statistics and performance data that can guide their decisions in real-time.

However, the human element of scouting remains vital. The ability to interpret data, assess a player’s potential based on observations, and understand the nuances of the game is irreplaceable. In the near future, successful football scouts will have a balanced skill set that combines analytical prowess with traditional game knowledge.

Football scouting is an exciting, rewarding field that plays a crucial role in the development of future stars. By understanding the processes and attributes scouts seek, both aspiring players and fans can appreciate the art involved in identifying talent on the pitch.

The next time you watch a match or hear about a promising young player, you’ll understand just how much effort goes into identifying stars in the making. As the landscape of football evolves, so too will the methods scouts use, but the ultimate goal will always remain the same: to find the next football sensation.

**Title:** Player Evaluations in Soccer: Key Metrics and Methods

Player evaluations are a crucial part of soccer, helping coaches, scouts, and analysts assess individual performance effectively. These evaluations not only gauge a player’s current abilities but also predict future potential. In this blog, we will explore the importance of player evaluations, key performance metrics, evaluation methods, and how data and technology enhance this process.

## **Why Are Player Evaluations in Soccer Important?**

Player evaluations offer valuable insights into a player’s strengths, weaknesses, and overall contributions to the team. Importantly, they help in several areas:

*   **Team Selection:** Coaches can make informed decisions about starting lineups and substitutions.
*   **Talent Identification:** Scouts use evaluations to discover promising players.
*   **Player Development:** Personalized training programs are created based on evaluation results.
*   **Transfers and Contracts:** Clubs assess players’ market value and potential investments.

Clearly, player evaluations are not just about performance but also about planning for the future.

## **Key Components of Player Evaluations**

### 1. **Technical Skills**

A player’s technical ability is essential for their effectiveness on the pitch. For example, key skills include:

*   **Ball Control:** The ability to receive and control the ball under pressure.
*   **Passing:** Accuracy, vision, and the ability to execute different types of passes.
*   **Shooting:** Power, accuracy, and technique in finishing.
*   **Dribbling:** The ability to beat defenders and retain possession.
*   **Defensive Skills:** Tackling, intercepting, and blocking.

**Evaluation Tip:** Observe technical execution in various game situations, from open play to set pieces. Moreover, pay attention to consistency.

Social Sciences

### 2. **Tactical Awareness**

Tactical understanding is equally vital for decision-making and positioning. In particular, key areas include:

*   **Positioning:** Awareness of space and movement relative to teammates and opponents.
*   **Decision-Making:** Choosing the right option quickly under pressure.
*   **Game Reading:** Anticipating plays and reacting accordingly.
*   **Role Understanding:** Adherence to tactical instructions and the ability to adapt.

**Evaluation Tip:** Focus on off-the-ball movement and how players interpret tactical roles. Additionally, consider their communication with teammates.

### 3. **Physical Attributes**

Physical capabilities often determine how well a player can execute their skills. Specifically, key attributes include:

*   **Speed:** Acceleration and sprinting ability.
*   **Strength:** Physicality in duels and shielding the ball.
*   **Endurance:** The ability to maintain performance levels throughout the match.
*   **Agility:** Quick changes in direction and balance.
*   **Injury Resilience:** Frequency and severity of injuries.

**Evaluation Tip:** Use fitness tests and GPS data to track physical performance metrics. In addition, monitor recovery rates after matches.

### 4. **Mental Attributes**

Mental toughness and psychological traits often separate good players from great ones. For instance, key attributes include:

*   **Focus and Concentration:** Staying alert and engaged throughout the match.
*   **Composure:** Staying calm under pressure, especially in critical moments.
*   **Leadership:** Motivating teammates and organizing play.
*   **Work Ethic:** Commitment to training and match preparation.
*   **Resilience:** Bouncing back from setbacks and maintaining confidence.

**Evaluation Tip:** Assess how players handle high-pressure situations and their response to mistakes. Furthermore, observe their body language.

### 5. **Statistical Performance Metrics**

Quantitative data provides objective insights into performance. Important metrics include:

*   **Passing Accuracy:** The percentage of successful passes.
*   **Shot Conversion Rate:** Goals per shots taken.
*   **Tackles and Interceptions:** Defensive contributions.
*   **Key Passes:** Passes leading to scoring opportunities.
*   **Expected Goals (xG):** The quality of chances created or taken.

**Evaluation Tip:** Use data analytics tools to track and analyze these metrics over time. Moreover, compare performance across different opponents.

## **Methods of Player Evaluations in Soccer**

### 1. **Match Observation**

Watching players in live matches or through video footage offers valuable insights into their performance in real-game situations.

Soccer nutrition plans

*   **Key Focus Areas:** Movement off the ball, decision-making, and execution under pressure.
*   **Tools:** Video analysis software like **Hudl** and **Wyscout** for reviewing match footage.

Additionally, note how players interact with teammates and adapt to changing scenarios.

### 2. **Training Assessments**

Evaluating players during training sessions helps identify their work ethic, adaptability, and response to coaching.

*   **Key Focus Areas:** Skill drills, tactical exercises, and fitness sessions.
*   **Tip:** Use drills that simulate match conditions for more accurate assessments. Furthermore, observe players’ willingness to learn and improve.

### 3. **Fitness and Physical Testing**

Physical tests measure attributes like speed, endurance, and agility.

*   **Common Tests:** 40-yard dash (speed), beep test (endurance), and agility ladders.
*   **Tools:** GPS trackers and heart rate monitors for precise data.

Notably, physical data should be combined with performance metrics for a holistic evaluation.

### 4. **Psychological Assessments**

Mental strength and personality traits can be evaluated through psychological profiling and observation.

Social Sciences

*   **Key Traits:** Leadership, resilience, and focus.
*   **Tip:** Consider using questionnaires and personality tests to gather insights. In addition, speak with coaches and teammates for feedback.

### 5. **Data and Analytics**

Data-driven analysis provides a comprehensive view of a player’s performance through metrics and visualizations.

*   **Key Tools:** Platforms like **Opta**, **StatsBomb**, and **InStat** for advanced metrics.
*   **Tip:** Combine data analysis with qualitative observations for a more balanced evaluation.

Furthermore, consider trends over multiple matches rather than isolated performances.

## **The Role of Technology in Player Evaluations**

Technology has revolutionized player evaluations, offering more accurate and detailed insights. Key technological advancements include:

### 1. **GPS Tracking**

Tracks players’ movement, distance covered, and speed, offering insights into their physical performance. Additionally, it helps in injury prevention.

Personalized coaching plans

### 2. **Video Analysis Software**

Allows coaches to review and break down match footage, highlighting key moments and player actions.

### 3. **Data Analytics Platforms**

Provide statistical analysis and performance metrics to identify patterns and trends. Importantly, these platforms simplify complex data.

### 4. **Wearable Devices**

Devices like heart rate monitors and smartwatches track physiological data, helping assess fitness levels and recovery. Furthermore, they offer real-time feedback.

## **Case Study: Evaluating Lionel Messi**

Lionel Messi is often cited as a perfect example of an all-around player. Evaluating Messi involves:

*   **Technical Skills:** His dribbling, passing, and finishing are world-class.
*   **Tactical Awareness:** Messi’s positioning and vision allow him to exploit spaces effectively.
*   **Physical Attributes:** While not the fastest, his agility and balance are exceptional.
*   **Mental Strength:** His composure in critical moments and ability to lead by example are unmatched.
*   **Statistical Performance:** High goal-scoring and assist records, alongside outstanding passing accuracy and key passes.

Indeed, Messi’s evaluations consistently reflect his impact on every aspect of the game.

## **Conclusion – Player Evaluations in Soccer**

Player evaluations are essential for understanding performance and potential in soccer. By analyzing technical, tactical, physical, and mental attributes, coaches and analysts can make informed decisions about player development and team strategy. With advancements in technology and data analytics, evaluations are becoming more precise and impactful. Whether you’re a coach, scout, or player, mastering the art of player evaluations can significantly enhance your understanding and appreciation of the game.

**Source:** https://www.thisisamericansoccer.com/insider-secrets-how-soccer-scouts-discover-hidden-gems/
**Title:** Insider Secrets: How Soccer Scouts Discover Hidden Gems

**Ever wondered how soccer scouts unearth the next big soccer sensation?**While most of us only see the spectacular goals, they’re watching every touch, run, pass, and tackle to spot future stars.

Soccer scouting is part art part science, especially with all of the advanced player statistics available today. This isn’t about who stands out today—it’s about raw potential that can be developed into a top level player.

Stay with us as we spill the beans on the techniques that soccer scouts use to find tomorrow’s legends.

## The Art of Finding Potential

Spotting talent isn’t just a job or all reliant on numbers – there is an art to it. Scouts don’t just look for technical skills; they seek the spark of brilliance that signals a diamond in the rough.

Even in a sea of players, trained eyes follow tiny details, such as:

*   Reading the game and anticipating what opponents are going to do
*   Top-end potential – does the player have 1-2 attributes that are undoubtedly at the top level?

It’s about seeing beyond the present and envisioning a player’s future—something like a football fortune teller!

## Beyond the Pitch: Scouting in Unlikely Places

Not all stars are found in packed stadiums. Some gems emerge from local tournaments or smaller clubs.

Scouts frequent these types of local games, usually with the tip of someone in their network, to watch a young player and see if they have the ability for a trial. Then they’ll invite these players to train with the club to see if they can step up to the challenge.

When I was playing in Germany, every few days there would be a local player or two brought in from a scout. Sometimes they’d step up and stick around, and other times they just weren’t quite good enough. But the scouts have to cast a wide net to find these diamonds in the rough – given a certain level of ability, you never know who will step up.

It’s a treasure hunt where every corner pitch might hold a soccer prodigy just waiting for recognition.

## Key Attributes of a Future Soccer Star

Speed and agility get noticed instantly, but mental attributes make the difference.

Scouts assess a player’s decision-making under pressure and their willingness to learn from mistakes.

They’re searching for those who can lead and inspire a team—not just on the field but in spirit too. These are attributes you can see in a competitive environment.

## The Psychological Edge: What Scouts Look For

You can’t measure determination on a stat sheet. Players aspiring for greatness shine not just with skill but with unmatched perseverance.

Scouts gauge resilience—how players tackle setbacks and recover from defeats.

A future star needs grit, fire, and hunger for victory, qualities that echo long after the game ends. At the highest level, the mental side of the game is a huge difference-maker.

For instance, Clint Dempsey always had a mental edge. Some thought he was over-confident on the field, but this confidence gave him the edge as he overcame the many challenges American players face grinding out a career at the top level in Europe. Without his swagger, it’s likely he never would have had the career he did.

## Game IQ: The Mind Is As Vital As the Body

Imagine a player who reads the game like a chess master. Scouts love players who exhibit strong soccer intelligence.

A player’s ability to strategize and outthink opponents reveals a vast understanding of the game. Making good, quick decisions in a game signals a player who is ready to step up to the next level.

In the chess game of soccer, the brains behind the boots are often the biggest difference-makers. Especially when you turn pro – you have to be able to learn quickly to advance to the next level.

## How Technology Has Revolutionized Scouting

Scouts utilize data to spot talent like never before.

With video analysis tools and AI assessments, the potential of players is scrutinized in detail. Sites like Opta, Whoscored, Fbref, and many others give scouts an indication of a player’s profile before they even set eyes on the player. While you can’t judge a soccer player by numbers alone (as opposed to some other sports), it can be a useful indicator to start the search.

It’s easier than ever for players themselves to compile highlight videos and send them to scouts.

Technology complements the personal touch, adding precision to what used to rely on instinct alone.

## Networking Within the Soccer Community

Scouts don’t work in isolation; they foster networks that extend around the globe.

Local coaches, former players, and even rival scouts share tidbits and tips. So many of the top clubs have scouts in every country. With the price of buying established players continuing to climb, it pays to find these players before they become prohibitively expensive.

## The Role of Academy Programs in Scouting

Academies are breeding grounds for talent, offering structured environments to hone skills. While there is a debate about whether or not the US academies are structured properly, there’s no doubt that professional academies all over the world churn out top level talent consistently.

Top-level scouts find these well-established academies as a great resource for the best up and coming players. It’s hard to stand out in these competitive environments, so those that do definitely show that they can rise to the ocassion.

## Evaluating Athleticism in Players

Physical attributes like speed, endurance, balance, and strength are crucial to scouts.

Scouts attend training sessions to observe fitness levels and agility during gameplay.

While soccer players must have more than raw athleticism, this trait can often determine a player’s ceiling – the higher the ceiling the more intriguing the prospect. Speed can often be the difference-maker at the highest levels.

## The Importance of Character and Conduct

Character assessments often accompany talent judgments. Scouts look for humility and cooperation.

Off-field behavior is just as crucial as on-field performance—the true professionalism breeds trust.

A player’s character often determines their long-term success as much as their abilities, so scouts must take a player’s personality into account before offering them a trial.

## Spotting Diamonds in International Talent Pools

As soccer is a global sport, U.S. scouts widen their searches internationally.

Watching competitions in Europe, Africa, and South America can provide diverse technical styles and skills.

The blend of cultures enriches the game, and developing talent from all over the world gives teams a leg up. Even Premier League teams and clubs like Real Madrid are bringing in younger players from all over the world to supplement their local talent pool.

## Nuturing Relationships with Young Prospects

Scouting isn’t just about discovering talent; it’s about fostering potential.

Building trust and guiding young stars through their growth journey is essential.

This mentorship ensures talented individuals stay focused and develop into the stars they’re meant to be.

## Advice for Aspiring Players

For rising stars, staying true to oneself is paramount. Embrace both strengths and weaknesses as opportunities for growth.

Regular practice and resilience in the face of adversity are non-negotiable elements.

Never stop learning, whether from mentors, teammates, or the game itself—curiosity fuels progress!

## Critical Takeaways for Effective Scouting

Success in scouting hinges on balance—the mix of art and science.

Scouts decode the human element behind the stats, numbers, and play tactics.

This fusion is what makes scouting such a pivotal yet thrilling endeavor in the world of soccer.

## Join the Discussion: How Would You Spot Talent?

We’ve peeled back the curtain, but we know you’ve got thoughts! How would you spot talent?

Are there attributes we missed, or players in your community with the potential to go pro?

Share your insights and stories below. After all, the next superstar could be the one you shine the light on!

**Bart Creasman**

Former professional soccer player and current soccer dad. Lifetime enthusiast of the game interested in sharing deeper insights into interesting topics about soccer.

**Title:** How to Identify Hidden Talents

## Scouting

### How to Identify Hidden Talents

April 11, 2024 by Guilherme Penna

**Introduction:** In the relentless pursuit of talent in the world of football, there is a crucial task that challenges scouts and talent spotters: identifying hidden talents, those underestimated or lesser-known players who have the potential to become stars. In this article, we will explore some fundamental strategies and techniques for identifying these hidden talents and unlocking their true potential in the field of football scouting. **1. Extensive Research:** One of the keys to identifying hidden talents is to conduct extensive and thorough research. This involves watching a wide variety of games, from top-level competitions to regional and youth leagues. By expanding the scope of research, scouts have a better chance of discovering talented players who may have gone unnoticed by traditional recruiters. **2. Observation of Specific Characteristics:** In addition to watching games, it is important to closely observe the specific characteristics of players. This includes not only technical skills such as ball control and passing accuracy but also physical traits such as speed, strength, and endurance. Furthermore, it is essential to assess the mentality and behavior of players on the field, including their determination, tactical intelligence, and ability to handle pressure. **3. Analysis of Relevant Statistics:** Statistics also play a crucial role in identifying hidden talents. By analyzing relevant statistics such as goals scored, assists, passing accuracy rates, and defensive effectiveness, scouts can identify performance patterns and distinguish players who excel in specific areas of the game. Advanced metrics such as expected goals (xG) and expected assists (xA) can also provide valuable insights into a player's true impact on the field. **4. Deep Investigation:** In addition to on-field observation and statistical analysis, it is important to conduct deeper investigations into players identified as hidden talents. This may include interviews with coaches, teammates, and other football professionals who can provide insights into the player's potential and character. Furthermore, it is helpful to investigate the player's background, including their academic background, medical history, and off-field behavior. **5. Confidence and Intuition:** Finally, it is essential to trust one's own judgment and intuition when identifying hidden talents in the field of football scouting. The most obvious players are not always the best, and it often requires courage to invest in lesser-known players with exceptional potential. Having confidence in your evaluation ability and making decisions based on a deep understanding of the game can lead to the discovery of truly extraordinary talents. **Conclusion:** Identifying hidden talents in the field of football scouting is an art that requires dedication, skill, and a holistic approach. By conducting extensive research, closely observing player characteristics, analyzing relevant statistics, conducting deep investigations, and trusting intuition, scouts have the opportunity to discover the next big names in football and help them achieve their true potential in the world of sports.

**Title:** The Hidden World of Soccer Scouts: How Talent is Discovered

You feel compelled to support great writing…

Before every soccer superstar, there stands a scout who recognized their potential long before everyone else. Soccer scouts are highly confidential hunters. They are hired to dig out talent from places that seem the most uninviting. Be it a teenager playing on a rugged field or a hopeful youth academy player, the scouts’ goal is to find the next big thing, and they surely do find it. But how? What makes an athlete distinct enough to qualify as a legend? The answers are concealed within soccer scouting. Let’s dig deeper!

Soccer scouts are perhaps the most underrated figures in the development of talent. They work tirelessly to witness, travel, and analyze hundreds, if not thousands, of players to identify the ones who have the finest chances of being successful achievers. Unlike coaches, who work with specific teams, scouts are focused on finding the best athletes out there. After all, you want future matches to be even better than the current ones, right? We are sure that you do, and without scouts and coaches, this would be impossible. And if you want to place bets on the nearest future matches, visit MelBet. There, you will find hundreds of betting options, and you can even study the statistics and historical data of many teams to make more thoughtful decisions. But let’s get back to the scouts.

While some scouts look at children who are as young as 12 and show elite potential, others target the more professional leagues in search of undervalued players. Good scouts do not only look at goals and assists but also at a player’s movement, mentality, and how they make decisions when under pressure. Unlike others, junior scouts have an eagle-like vision that lets them spot astounding talent many years in advance.

Scouting is not merely watching matches. It also involves examining certain details that indicate future success. Here are the most important aspects scouts look for:

1.   Technical Ability: Direct ball control, dribbling, didactic passing, and the accuracy of the first touch define the pressure under which a player can operate.
2.   Game Intelligence: Tactical awareness and movement off the ball, along with decision-making, show whether a player has a broader understanding of the game beyond just abilities.
3.   Physical Attributes: Speed, endurance, strength, and agility help scouts determine whether a player possesses the physique to compete at higher levels.
4.   Mentality and Work Ethic: Confidence, resilience, adaptability, and more prove whether a player has the fighter’s mind and attitude that are needed to flourish in a professional environment.

The process of scouting used to require a lot of time and was based on one’s experience and observation. However, the process has transformed as data analytics has come along. Clubs have a wide range of new technologies that allow them to analyze every aspect of a player’s game, from completion rates to how fast a player can sprint or how much pressure he puts on defense.

Scouts now merge traditional eye tests with statistical calculations to come up with more informed decisions. Even if a player does not stand out from the crowd, if the data indicates that they are able to make attempts toward scoring goals, they are put on the radar. Moreover, AI-enabled video tracking makes it easier for scouts to monitor players and discover talent all over the globe. Even though videos cannot replace scouting, they allow scouts to gain insight with greater efficiency and on a larger scale.

No single scout is on their own. For all the top clubs, the strongest and best players on the field are guaranteed not to be overlooked, thanks to video scouts from different countries and leagues. These teams are always in sync with regional scouts, video scouts, and recruitment directors, meaning reports and information can be exchanged continuously.

The leading clubs in Europe have partnered with the top academies across South America, Africa, and Asia to ensure that no single club can monopolize any region’s top young prospects. In South America, clubs need to rush and find the next wunderkind during youth competitions before European clubs snatch them up. Having a strong scouting network not only identifies talent but also allows clubs to have many young prospects sign with them before they become exponentially richer and more famous.

Looking for players is hard, particularly in under-covered leagues. Scouts looking in these unknown places have to go through:

*   Tough Conditions: Certain leagues have no reliable video coverage, so scouts have to go in person.
*   Quality of Opponents: A player could be a beast in a lower league, but will he be able to make it against top-tier opposition?
*   Language and Cultural Barriers: A player’s attributes go beyond their field skills and incorporate elements such as their background, culture, and character.
*   Overlooked Potential from Major Teams: Certain players mature later in life. Scouts need to see the potential, especially when the player is not enrolled in a high-class academy.

However, scouts who are successful in these places have endless value, as they are able to find the most talent. These types of players are world-class but unknown.

Soccer scouting is an innovation that has almost increased overnight. Thanks to scouting software analytics, AI-supported analysis, cloud-based platforms, and global database access, clubs have massive resources at their disposal. Yet, no algorithm can supersede a scout’s trained eye that captures the unseen. The future belongs to the scouts who know how to combine technology with intuition and attempt to look for talent in places where nobody else is looking. The next superstar lies in waiting somewhere on a dusty pitch!

**Source:** https://ouarsenis.com/football-academies-that-are-changing-the-game-the-secrets-of-ajax-barca-and-sporting/
**Title:** Football Academies That Are Changing the Game

## Football Academies That Are Changing the Game: The Secrets of Ajax, Barça, and Sporting

Football academies are the hidden engines behind every footballing powerhouse. While the glory goes to the stars on the pitch, it is the academies that forge raw talent into world-class athletes. Among the most influential are Ajax, FC Barcelona, and Sporting CP — three academies that don’t just produce players, but redefine the sport itself. This article explores the secret ingredients of their success, analyzes the systems that nurture greatness, and examines why their methods are shaping the future of global football.

## The Philosophy Behind Player Development

### Cultivating Identity Through Methodical Training

At the heart of every successful football academy lies a philosophy that transcends simple training. For Ajax, it’s **“Total Football”** — a fluid, high-IQ style rooted in the teachings of Rinus Michels and Johan Cruyff. At Barcelona, the ideology is **“tiki-taka”**, a short-passing, high-possession approach cultivated through La Masia. Sporting CP emphasizes a **technical and tactical schooling** tailored to both the Portuguese domestic game and international demands.

Each of these academies ingrains their philosophy from the earliest youth levels, ensuring consistency and cohesion across all age groups. Children at Ajax start learning positional awareness and the importance of spatial exploitation before they even enter full-pitch training. La Masia instills discipline, humility, and an obsession with the ball — the idea that every movement must have a purpose. Sporting CP, meanwhile, blends physicality with skill, preparing its players to thrive either in Portugal or abroad.

## Tactical Intelligence from a Young Age

### The Emphasis on Mental Speed and Awareness

What sets these academies apart from the average is their commitment to developing **football intelligence** as early as possible. Players aren’t simply taught how to pass or shoot — they’re taught **why**, **when**, and **what comes next**.

At Ajax, “brain training” is a formal part of the curriculum. Small-sided games with specific rules are used to simulate real-match scenarios. One such example is the “Rondo”, a positional game that is central to both Ajax and Barça’s academies. It’s more than a warm-up — it’s a tool to develop anticipation, pressing traps, and quick decision-making.

Barcelona takes this a step further by incorporating game theory and pattern recognition into youth drills. Players are trained to understand triangles, numerical superiority, and time-space dynamics. Sporting CP’s scouts and coaches emphasize vision and “anticipatory play,” training athletes not just to react, but to read plays before they unfold.

## Data-Driven Coaching and Monitoring

### Tracking Progress, Not Just Results

In the modern football landscape, **technology and data analytics** have infiltrated even grassroots levels. These academies are early adopters of sports science and digital monitoring to track progress and reduce injury risk.

At Ajax, wearable devices are used to measure biometrics during both training and recovery. La Masia has partnered with AI-based platforms to assess movement patterns, while Sporting uses a proprietary evaluation model to assess more than 60 criteria per player annually.

Here is a compact overview of how these academies integrate technology in youth development:

| Academy | Data Usage Focus | Tools Employed | Outcome Measured |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ajax | Biometric tracking, GPS metrics | Catapult, heart-rate monitors | Endurance, positional awareness |
| FC Barcelona | Tactical simulation | AI-assisted video analysis, SmartBall tech | Decision-making under pressure |
| Sporting CP | Performance analytics | Internal evaluation system, scout grids | Tactical fit, progression markers |

These systems don’t just track speed or goals; they allow coaches to **personalize** development pathways for each player, focusing on long-term potential rather than short-term results.

## Talent Identification and Scouting Networks

### Local Roots and Global Reach

One of the most striking commonalities among these academies is how well they **identify talent** — not just from their local regions but globally.

Ajax maintains a dense scouting network across the Netherlands and increasingly across Africa and Scandinavia. Their partnership with satellite clubs in Cape Town and Helsinki has provided a steady pipeline of raw talent. FC Barcelona, despite a focus on Catalonia, has widened its net across South America and Asia — but never at the expense of local culture.

Sporting CP’s reputation in Portugal is unmatched. Their scouts are trained to spot not only physical aptitude but also **emotional resilience** and psychological maturity, key traits for long-term development. Unlike many academies that prioritize early bloomers, Sporting looks for **late developers**, often hidden in plain sight.

In this context, their approach to scouting revolves around the following key steps:

*   **Technical observation:** Ball control, dribbling style, and decision-making are prioritized over size or strength.

*   **Background screening:** Coaches meet families, assess socioeconomic context, and understand personal challenges.

*   **Psychological profiling:** Players are evaluated for discipline, adaptability, and self-motivation, especially critical for Sporting CP.

By investing as much in people as in players, these academies build loyalty and longevity into the system.

## Educational and Personal Development

### Building Humans Before Athletes

Great footballers aren’t just born with talent — they are **mentored**, **educated**, and shaped. All three academies enforce **rigorous educational standards** and psychological development programs.

La Masia famously requires its young athletes to study full-time alongside their training. Tutors and psychologists are embedded in the academy infrastructure. Ajax’s “Plan Cruyff” ensures that every youth player attends school and participates in community service. Sporting CP offers life-skills seminars ranging from nutrition to personal finance.

In many cases, the **character** of the academy graduate becomes a marketable attribute. Clubs around the world trust that an Ajax or Barça-trained player is likely to be disciplined, tactically aware, and socially mature.

This holistic approach also reduces dropout trauma. Those who don’t make it to the professional level often transition into coaching, sports science, or administration — a testament to how well-rounded the education really is.

## Signature Players and Alumni Influence

### From the Academy to Global Stardom

No analysis is complete without acknowledging the **hall of fame** that each of these academies has produced.

Ajax has gifted the world with Dennis Bergkamp, Clarence Seedorf, Wesley Sneijder, and more recently, Frenkie de Jong and Matthijs de Ligt. FC Barcelona’s La Masia is the birthplace of Xavi, Iniesta, Busquets, and the one and only Lionel Messi. Sporting CP gave the game Cristiano Ronaldo, Luís Figo, João Moutinho, and Bruno Fernandes.

But it’s not just individual brilliance — these alumni often **return** to mentor, coach, or consult. Xavi now manages Barcelona. Ajax’s structure is influenced by former graduates like Edwin van der Sar and Marc Overmars. At Sporting, former players serve as regional scouts and academy directors.

Their presence reinforces identity and ensures that new generations remain rooted in the culture that shaped them.

## Why the Model Works — and What Others Can Learn

### Adaptability, Structure, and Patience

The real secret? **Stability meets innovation**. These academies are not stuck in the past — they evolve constantly, balancing heritage with progress. Ajax has overhauled its curriculum multiple times to integrate modern training. Barça has adopted sports psychology as a core pillar. Sporting CP now integrates neuro-tracking and vision training to enhance split-second decision-making.

Their models work because they resist the temptation of short-term wins. They understand that greatness takes time. Their systems are not dependent on one coach or philosophy but are **modular**, **scalable**, and **self-sustaining**.

The most successful elements that other academies worldwide can emulate include:

*   Embedding philosophy early, not just in the top tier.

*   Focusing on **mental skills** and not just technical drills.

*   Establishing **feedback loops** between coaches, data analysts, and educators.

*   Preparing players for life, not just sport.

It’s no wonder that federations and clubs from Asia to South America are now modeling their systems on the blueprints of Ajax, Barça, and Sporting.

## Conclusion

These academies don’t just teach football — they build futures. The legacies of Ajax, FC Barcelona, and Sporting CP are deeply embedded in the global game, not only through the stars they’ve produced but in how they’ve raised the bar for what football education can be. Their holistic, innovative, and identity-driven approaches are a masterclass in sustainable excellence — one that will continue to shape football for decades to come.

**Source:** https://www.nytimes.com/athletic/4901460/2023/09/27/ajax-crisis-mislintat/
**Title:** How Ajax imploded

Expeditie Robinson is a reality television show that has been running in the Netherlands for the past 23 years, where contestants are marooned in a remote part of the world and challenged to ‘survive’.

In 2021, Jan van Halst appeared on the show. An ex-footballer with a modest career behind him, he had since established himself as a match analyser with Ziggo Sport, a Dutch television channel, while co-running a business that teaches leadership development.

Though he would gain administrative experience in football at FC Twente, it was a surprise when, at the end of May, Van Halst was added to Ajax’s supervisory board, which had only been reformed two months earlier.

At the start of September, it was even more of a surprise as he emerged as the club’s interim chief executive.

A former midfielder, Van Halst is not one of Ajax’s most famous names. In three years, between 1999 and 2002, he played just a handful of games for the club. Yet he has now emerged as both the spokesman and perhaps the symbol of the acute problems that exist at a club that feels like it is undergoing the most intense of identity crises.

Jan van Halst has had a turbulent time at Ajax Raymond Smit/NESImages/DeFodi Images via Getty Images

On Sunday, frustrations and anger that had been bubbling for months finally boiled over. Feyenoord were the visitors to the Johan Cruyff Arena for ‘De Klassieker’ — Dutch football’s biggest fixture and a fraught occasion at the best of times. Ajax, having started the day perilously close to the Eredivisie relegation zone, were 1-0 down after nine minutes. Nine minutes later, that became 2-0, and 3-0 shortly before half-time.

The mood was already sour, with fan protests against the board — which, until his promotion, Van Halst was briefly a part of. Initially, this involved simply displaying banners and chanting but, shortly into the second half, projectiles (including fireworks) were thrown onto the pitch, endangering the safety of players.

Ajax fans throw flares onto the field during the Feyenoord match Olaf Kraak/ANP/AFP

The teams were taken off the field and the match was abandoned. Afterwards, riot police had to disperse supporters as they tried to regain access to the stadium, with glass doors being smashed.

While mayhem took hold outside, Van Halst appeared in the stadium’s press room fresh from a crisis meeting with other executives.

The problem is that the 54-year-old does not control strategy at Ajax. As the Dutch journalist Sjoerd Mossou wrote the following day for the Dutch newspaper Algemeen Dagblad: “No one is really the boss at the club at the moment — and at the same time, everyone is a little bit.”

Riot police confront Ajax fans Olaf Kraak/ANP/AFP

There is no shortage of targets for Ajax fan frustration, but prime amongst them was Sven Mislintat.

The German sporting director, like Van Halst, was only appointed to his position in May after rebuilding his reputation at Stuttgart. Though Mislintat had been credited with helping design Jurgen Klopp’s great Borussia Dortmund side, he would leave Arsenal in 2019 after two years with a transfer record that was less impressive. His appointment also raised eyebrows among some of Ajax’s rivals, who were surprised that the club had decided to hire a man who was renowned for having a volatile temperament.

With chaos reigning on the pitch and in the stands against Feyenoord, Ajax had to act from within and, after less than five months, Mislintat’s time in Amsterdam was over.

Though a statement on the club’s website initially suggested that a parting of the ways was agreed mutually, that sentence was later removed.

“It is now in the interest of the club to find the sporting path back up as quickly as possible with united forces,” Van Halst reflected, somewhat optimistically.

De Telegraaf, another Dutch newspaper, had reported a few days earlier that, after Ajax had taken just five points from four Eredivisie games, Mislintat had entered the first-team dressing room, threatening in front of players to sack Maurice Steijn, the coach he had helped appoint.

Mislintat (left) and Ajax head coach Maurice Steijn Phil Nijhuis/ANP/AFP via Getty Images

Steijn was not present during this alleged tirade, which some players pushed back against.

Yet there were other reasons why Mislintat’s position had quickly become untenable. An investigation into his transfer activities had started on 20 September after a summer where the legendary Marco van Basten suggested that the club did not resemble Ajax. “It’s more FC Amsterdam,” he suggested.

After finishing third and missing out on Champions League qualification by six points last season, a hole in Ajax’s budget meant the club had to sell players first to create the economy for new signings.

Mislintat would oversee deals for 24 players in total: 11 sales and 13 signings. Several key negotiations would take time to reach conclusions. While Jurrien Timber and Calvin Bassey were sold early in the summer to Arsenal and Fulham respectively, Edson Alvarez and Mohammed Kudus would only agree terms with West Ham United in the final stages of the transfer window.

In total, Milslintat raised €160million (£139.1m; $168.2m), with the experienced Davy Klaassen and Dusan Tadic also departing on free transfers. Mislintat would spend nearly half of that on recruits who have not yet proven themselves at the highest level. Half of that outlay, indeed, went on three players from the second tiers of foreign leagues, including Chuba Akpom from Middlesbrough, who barely 12 months ago was on the transfer list at the Championship club and struggling to find a new team.

Top League Content

Milslintat was criticised throughout the summer for his strategy. He would spend €8million, for example, on Diant Ramaj, a 21-year-old goalkeeper from Eintracht Frankfurt, whose experience in the Bundesliga over the past two seasons amounted to just two games.

It had seemed that Ramaj would get his chance in the Ajax first team after the intended first-choice goalkeeper Geronimo Rulli was injured shortly after his arrival, but Steijn instead chose Jay Gorter, who was marked to go out on loan having spent the second half of last term struggling for game time at Aberdeen in the Scottish Premiership.

Ajax’s bad start to the campaign intensified the talk around Mislintat’s work. Last Wednesday, the Dutch broadcaster NOS revealed that it had looked into one of Mislintat’s moves in the last hours of the transfer window, when he sanctioned an €8million deal for Croatian left-back Borna Sosa from his former club, Stuttgart.

Sosa’s transfer from Stuttgart to Ajax caused controversy Alexander Hassenstein/Getty Images

This, according to NOS, passed through the office of a German agency, which owns shares in a private company that Mislintat founded four years ago.

It is alleged that shares at Matchmetrics GmbH, a commercial data system for scouting players, were initially divided equally between four investors, but five weeks after Mislintat was appointed at Ajax, he became the largest shareholder, with 35 per cent.

After a loan of €161,000 was converted into three per cent of the shares, agents Arthur Beck and Kerim Cerit became direct business partners of Mislintat, who shook hands with the pair to recruit Sosa.

Ajax said in a statement that it was “already aware” of Mislintat’s shareholding in Matchmetrics, adding that “contractual agreements were made about this at the time”, but that it did not know about the agency buying shares in Matchmetrics.

It had launched its own investigation into the matter last Wednesday but, in the statement confirming Mislintat’s sacking, insisted that the decision to fire him was “unrelated” to that probe.

Though Mislintat is gone from Ajax and it might seem as though the club has reached rock bottom, there remains a feeling that things could get worse before they get better.

The focus has now switched to the figures who brought Mislintat to the club. Pressure, especially, has fallen on the recently assembled supervisory board, which currently does not have a general manager to report to in the absence of Edwin van der Sar, who left his role last summer and is currently recovering from a brain haemorrhage suffered while on holiday in Croatia in July.

Alex Kroes is scheduled to replace Van der Sar, but only in March. Kroes has his own links to the agency business having co-founded SEG, one of the biggest companies of its kind in Europe, although he left in 2018.

For the time being, Steijn has avoided a landslide of criticism as he is viewed as a symptom of Ajax’s problems rather than the cause. “Steijn is a good coach,” said Ton, an Ajax fan who did not want his surname to be used, “but he’s not used to working at the top level. The pressure at every game is on during the game, but also during the week.

“You can’t blame him because the other coach went away and in two weeks he was the coach. Everyone asked: ‘Is he the right coach for Ajax?’ I don’t think he’s the right coach but I can’t blame him for the players he has to work with. That’s the work of a (sporting) director.”

Later today, he is expected to lead the team as it completes the remaining half an hour of the game against Feyenoord, with Dutch law stipulating that this fixture has to be completed within 72 hours of when it was halted — a point that has not thrilled FC Volendam, who were due to face Ajax on Wednesday evening and were fancying their chances against out-of-sorts opponents.

Feyenoord, meanwhile, are still smarting from having what should have been one of their most memorable victories overshadowed by Sunday’s scenes. There are also concerns more broadly about the precedent that might be set for rogue supporters if, somehow, Ajax manage to salvage something in the time remaining in the match on Wednesday.

Repairs are made to broken windows at the main entrance of the Johan Cruyff Arena Koen van Weel/ANP/AFP

In moments like these, Ajax usually turns to the past for answers and former player Rafael van der Vaart thinks it is time for Louis van Gaal to return to the club.

Van der Vaart says that he recently had lunch with Van Gaal, who was supposedly “itching” for another shot at Ajax, where he made his name as a coach.

Publicly, Van Gaal has told a different story in the recent past, indicating that he is not interested in a role at Ajax due to his own health concerns.

For Ajax, these are huge administrative challenges that date back to February 2022, when Marc Overmars left his role as the director of football following a newspaper investigation that revealed a “series of inappropriate messages to several female colleagues”, according to a formal statement by the club.

In that vacuum, technical managers Gerry Hamstra and Klaas Jan Huntelaar formed part of a team responsible for the club’s transfer policy. Hamstra departed in April after the failure of several signings, including a deal for the Argentine Lucas Ocampos as a replacement for Manchester United-bound Antony. The winger’s loan agreement was terminated early so as not to activate any sale option.

Van der Sar was cornered by fans when attempting to leave the Johan Cruyff Arena after a previous defeat to Feyenoord in March. Supporters vented their frustrations at the club’s apparent decline to the then CEO and demanded he bring Overmars back.

“When Overmars was technical director and Van der Sar worked together, they had a strong team, they bought good players and also sold them for a lot of money,” said Ajax fan Ton. “They had a good balance in their financial view. Since he’s gone, it’s going downwards, downwards, downwards. No one is strong enough to make decisions.”

It has become clearer, however, that Overmars created minimal formal apparatus in the space he left behind. Rather than employ proper scouting and database management to keep an eye on players, Overmars conducted a lot of work via WhatsApp, using his name and profile as a player to open doors rather than ensure those channels were open to others at Ajax.

Mislintat was supposed to bring new data-led thinking to a club where a more traditional approach has flourished. Last year saw the appointment of Maurits Hendriks as the club’s chief sports officer, an entirely new but undefined role.

While Hendriks’ background is in field hockey rather than football, more traditional thinkers have also questioned the title of the recently hired Vosse de Boode as the club’s head of sport science and data analytics.

Amid all the turmoil, there are glimmers of optimism. Even some of Ajax’s rivals would concede that their squad has the necessary quality to win the Dutch title for the fourth time in six years — the club’s wage bill is significantly higher than Feyenoord’s, for example. Once the club’s culture is reset, the football should be able to take care of itself.

Off the field, too, the new supervisory board at least has recognised the club has an administrative problem. Under Overmars and Van der Sar, the football people ran the show and only football thinking mattered.

This was evident as the football men shuffled uncomfortably in the glare of serious allegations, unsure of how to manage them.

This has led to Ajax launching a two-year talent programme, in which former players spend time working in different departments ranging from commercial to legal, as well as media.

The first group of former players to participate in the program were Siem de Jong, Daniel de Ridder, Ricardo van Rhijn and Kelly Zeeman.

It will take time for any amount of hard work in this initiative to bear fruit, but at least it’s a start.

_Additional reporting: Adam Crafton and Charlotte Harpur_

_(Top photos: Getty Images; design: Eamonn Dalton)_
